Paragraph (2) 
The existence of special treatment in the form of Value Added Tax which 
is payable, but not collected, means that the Input Tax relating to the 
delivery of Taxable Goods and/or Taxable Services that receives the 
relevant special treatment can still be credited. Thus, Value Added Tax is 
still payable, but not collected 
Example: 
Taxable Entrepreneur A produces Taxable Goods that receives a facility 
from the state, namely the Value Added Tax payable on the delivery of 
Taxable Goods is not collected forever (not only postponed) 
To produce the Taxable Goods, Taxable Entrepreneur A uses other 
Taxable Goods and/or Taxable Services as raw materials, auxiliary 
materials, capital goods, or as another cost component. 
When purchasing other Taxable Goods and/or Taxable Services, Taxable 
Entrepreneur A pays the Value Added Tax to the Taxable Entrepreneur 
who sells or delivers such a Taxable Goods or Taxable Services. 
The Value Added Tax paid by Taxable Entrepreneur A to the supplier 
Taxable Entrepreneur is an Input Tax which can be credited with the 
Output Tax, Input Tax can still be credited with the Output Tax even 
though the Output Tax is nil due to enjoying the Value Added Tax facility 
that is not collected from the state based on provisions as referred to in 
paragraph (1) 
If the Value Added Tax paid by Taxable Entrepreneur A to the supplier 
Taxable Entrepreneur is an Input Tax that can be credited with the Output 
Tax, the Input Tax can still be credited with the Output Tax even though 
the Output Tax is nil due to enjoying the Value Added Tax facility that is 
not collected from the state based on the provisions as referred to in 
paragraph (1) 
Paragraph (3) 
Different from the provisions in paragraph (2), the existence of special 
treatment in the form of exemption from the Value Added Tax imposition 
results in the absence of Output Tax so that the Input Tax relating to the 
delivery of Taxable Goods and/or Taxable Services that receives the 
exemption cannot be credited. 
Example:

Taxable Entrepreneur B produces Taxable Goods that receives a facility

To produce such a Taxable Goods, Taxable Entrepreneur B uses other

When purchasing other Taxable Goods and/or Taxable Services, Taxable

Although the Value Added Tax paid by Taxable Entrepreneur B to the 
supplier Taxable Entrepreneur is an Input Tax which can be credited, 
because of the absence of Output Tax due to the tax exemption given as 
referred to in paragraph (1), the Input Tax cannot be credited 
 
Article 16C **) 
Value Added Tax is imposed on the self-building activities that are carried out 
not within the business activity or work by individuals or entities the outcomes of 
which are used by themselves or used by other parties the limitations and 
procedures of which shall be regulated by the Decree of the Minister of Finance. 
 
Elucidation of Article 16C 
Self-building activities that are not carried out within the business activity or 
work, are subject to the Value Added Tax on consideration to prevent the 
avoidance of Value Added Tax imposition. 
To protect the low-income people from the Value Added Tax imposition on the 
self-building activities, then, the limitations on the self-building activities shall be 
regulated by the Decree of the Minister of Finance. 
 
Article 16D***) 
Value Added Tax is imposed on the delivery of Taxable Goods in the form of 
assets which according to their original purpose are not for sale by the Taxable 
Entrepreneur, except on the delivery of assets of which the Input Tax cannot be 
credited as referred to in Article 9 paragraph (8) letter b and letter c 
 
Elucidation of Article 16D 
Delivery of Taxable Goods, such as, in the form of machineries, buildings, 
equipments, furniture, or other Taxable Goods which according to their original 
purpose are not for sale by the Taxable Entrepreneur, is subject to tax 
However, Value Added Tax is not imposed on the transfer of Taxable Goods that 
does not have a direct relationship with business activities and the transfer of 
assets which according to their original purpose are not for sale, namely motor 
vehicles in the form of sedans and station wagons, which according to the

Article 16E 
 
(1) 
Value Added Tax and Sales Tax on Luxury Goods that have been paid on 
the purchase of Taxable Goods that are brought out of the Customs Area 
by an individual who holds a foreign passport, can be applied for refund. 
***) 
(2) 
Value Added Tax and Sales Tax on Luxury Goods that can be applied for 
refund as referred to in paragraph (1) must meet the following 
requirements: ***) 
a. 
value of the Value Added Tax shall be at least Rp. 500.000,00 (five 
hundred thousand rupiah), and can be adjusted with the 
Government Regulation; 
b. 
the purchase of the Taxable Goods shall be made within 1 (one) 
month prior to departure outside the Customs Area; and 
c. 
The Tax Invoice shall comply with the provisions as referred to in 
Article 13 paragraph (5), except on the Taxpayer Identification 
Number column and the buyer's address shall be filled-in with the 
passport number and full address in the country issuing the 
passport,  on the sale to an individual foreign passport holder who 
does not have a Taxpayer Identification Number 
(3) 
The Request for refund of Value Added Tax and Sales Tax on Luxury 
Goods as referred to in paragraph (1) shall be made when the individual 
foreign passport holder leaves Indonesia and shall be submitted to the 
Director General of Taxes through the Office of the Directorate General of 
Taxes at the airport as determined by the Minister of Finance. ***) 
(4) 
Documents that must be shown when requesting for refund of Value 
Added Tax and Sales Tax on Luxury Goods are: ***) 
a. 
passport; 
b. 
boarding pass for departure of an individual person as referred to in 
paragraph (1) to outside the Customs Area; and 
c. 
Tax Invoice as referred to in paragraph (2) letter c 
(5) 
The provisions on the procedure for submission and settlement of the 
request for refund of Value Added Tax and Sales Tax on Luxury Goods as 
referred to in paragraph (1) shall be regulated by or based on the 
Regulation of the Minister of Finance. ***)

Article 16F 
 
Buyers of Taxable Goods or recipients of Taxable Services are jointly and 
severally responsible for the tax payments, provided that they cannot show a 
proof that the Tax has been paid. ***) 
 
Elucidation of Article 16F 
In accordance with the principle, the tax payment burden for the Value Added 
Tax on Goods and Services and the Sales Tax on Luxury Goods is on the buyer 
or consumer of goods or the recipients of services. Therefore, it is proper if the 
buyer or consumer of goods and the recipients of services are jointly and 
severally responsible for the payment of the tax payable if it is evident that the 
tax payable is uncollectible on the seller or the service provider and the buyer or 
service recipient cannot show a proof of having made a tax payment to the seller 
or the service provider

MISCELLANEOUS

Matters relating to the meaning and the procedure of collection in relation to the 
implementation of this Law, which have not been specifically regulated in this 
Law, shall be governed by the provisions in the Law on General Provisions and 
Procedures of Taxation and other statutory regulations.

Self-explanatory. 
 
CHAPTER VII 
TRANSITIONAL PROVISIONS  
 
Article 18 
 
(1) 
Upon the coming into effect of this Law: 
a. 
all Deliveries of Taxable Goods or Taxable Services and Imports of 
Taxable Goods that have been made before the coming into effect 
of this Law, shall remain subject to tax payable according to the 
Sales Tax Law 1951; 
b. 
as long as the implementing regulation of this law has not been 
issued, the implementing regulations are not contradictory to this 
law which have not been revoked and replaced, are declared still 
valid. 
(2) 
The implementing provisions as referred to in paragraph (1) shall be 
regulated further by the Minister of Finance

Paragraph (1) 
Letter a 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter b 
All of the existing implementing regulations, which are issued to the 
effect of implementation of the Sales Tax Law 1951, which are not 
contradictory to the substances and purposes of this law, shall 
remain in effect as long as not revoked and replaced yet with the 
implementing regulations issued under this Law. 
Paragraph (2) 
The provisions of this paragraph (2) are intended to overcome the 
difficulties that may arise during the transitional period as a result of the 
enactment of Law on Value Added Tax on Goods and Services and Sales 
Tax On Luxury Goods and the cessation from enforcement of the Sales 
Tax Law 1951, to the same imposition object, such as: 
- 
long-term contracts or contracts with validity period covering two 
terms of the laws as set-forth above; 
- 
the remaining Sales Price or Replacement that is unpaid; 
- 
inventory of goods on which the Input Tax is not existent yet 
In this case, the Minister of Finance shall have the authority to stipulate 
the implementing regulations other than those as referred to in paragraph 
(1), in order to reduce the injustice in tax imposition and to facilitate the 
implementation of this law.

PRESIDENT 
OF THE REPUBLIC OF INDONESIA 
 
COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT OF LAW OF THE REPUBLIC OF 
INDONESIA NUMBER 12 OF 1994 REGARDING AMENDMENT TO LAW 
NUMBER 12 OF 1985 REGARDING THE LAND AND BUILDING TAX  
 
CHAPTER I 
GENERAL PROVISIONS  
 
Article 1 
 
In this Law, the following definitions apply: 
1. 
Earth means the earth surface and the earth body beneath it; 
2. 
Building means a technical construction that is permanently planted or 
attached to the land and/or the waters; 
3. 
Tax Object Sales Value or Nilai Jual Obyek Pajak means the average price 
obtained from a sale and purchase transaction that occurs fairly, and if 
there is no sale and purchase transaction, the Tax Object Sales Value is 
determined through a price comparison with other similar objects, or a 
new acquisition value, or a Substitute Tax Object Sales Value; 
4. 
Tax Object Notification Letter or Surat Pemberitahuan Obyek Pajak means 
a letter used by taxpayers to report their tax object data according to the 
provisions of this law; 
5. 
Notification of Tax Due or Surat Pemberitahuan Pajak Terhutang means a 
letter used by the Directorate General of Taxes to notify the taxpayer of 
the amount of tax payable; 
 
Elucidation of Article 1 
Number 1 
The earth surface includes lands and inland waters and seas within the 
territory of Indonesia 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
292 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
Number 2 
Included in the definition of building are: 
- 
environment roads located within a building complex such as hotel, 
factory, and its emplacements and others which form integral part 
of the building complex; 
- 
TOLL road; 
- 
swimming pools; 
- 
luxury fence; 
- 
sport venues; 
- 
shipyards, docks; 
- 
luxury gardens; 
- 
oil, water and gas reservoir/refinery, oil pipelines; 
- 
other facilities that provide benefits; 
Number 3 
The following definitions apply: 
- 
Price comparison with other similar objects is an approach/method 
of determining the selling value of a tax object by comparing it with 
other similar tax objects in adjacent location and have the same 
function and its selling price has been known. 
- 
New acquisition value is an approach/method of determining the 
selling value of a tax object by calculating all costs incurred to 
acquire sun an object at the time of valuation, subtracted by the 
depreciation based on the physical condition of the object. 
- 
Substitute selling value, is an approach/method of determining the 
selling value of a tax object based on the production results of the 
tax object 
Number 4 
Self-explanatory. 
Number 5 
Self-explanatory.

Elucidation of Article 3 
Paragraph (1) 
Not intended for profit means that the tax object is endeavored to serve 
the public interest, and is really not intended to gain profit. This can be 
known, among other things, from the articles of association and the 
memorandum of association of a foundation/entity engaging in worship, 
social, health, educational, and national cultural sectors. This definition 
includes state-owned tourism forests in accordance with Article 2 of Law 
Number 5 of 1967 regarding Basic Provisions of Forestry 
Example: 
- 
Islamic boarding schools (pesantren) or the like; 
- 
madrasah; 
- 
waqf land; 
- 
general hospitals. 
Paragraph (2) 
Tax object in this paragraph means a tax object owned/controlled/used by 
the Central Government and Regional Governments in administering the 
government. Land and Building Tax is a state tax the most of revenues of 
which are regional revenues which are used, among other things, for 
provision of facilities which are also enjoyed by both the Central 
Government and the Regional Governments. 
Therefore, it is reasonable for the Central Government to also participate 
in financing the provision of these facilities through payments of Land and

Paragraph (3) 
For each Taxpayer, the Non-Taxable Tax Object Sales Value is given 
amounting to Rp. 8.000.000,00 (eight million rupiah). If a Taxpayer has

Objects are still subject to tax fully without being deducted by the Non-
Taxable Tax Object Sales Value.

CHAPTER III 
TAX SUBJECT 
 
Article 4 
 
(1) 
The tax subject is a person or entity who really has a right to the land, 
and/or obtains the benefit of the land, and/or owns, controls, and/or 
obtains the benefit of the building. 
(2) 
Any tax subject as referred to in paragraph (1) who is subject to 
obligation to pay the tax become a taxpayer according to this Law 
(3) 
In the event the taxpayer for a tax object is not known clearly, the 
Director General of Taxes may determine the tax subject as referred to in 
paragraph (1) to be the taxpayer. 
(4) 
The tax subject that is determined as referred to in paragraph (3) may 
give a written statement to the Director General of Taxes that he/she is 
not a taxpayer for the tax object in question. 
(5) 
If the statement submitted by the taxpayer as referred to in paragraph 
(4) is approved, the Director General of Taxes shall cancel such a 
determination as taxpayer as referred to in paragraph (3) within one 
month as of the its receipt of such a statement letter. 
(6) 
If the statement submitted is not approved, the Director General of Taxes 
shall issue a decision letter of rejection accompanied by the reasons 
thereof 
(7) 
If after the one month period as of the receipt date of the statement as 
referred to in paragraph (4), the Director General of Taxes does not issue 
any decision, then the statement submitted shall be deemed approved. 
 
Elucidation of Article 4 
Paragraph (1) 
A proof of payment/ settlement of tax is not a proof of right ownership 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
298 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
Paragraph (2) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (3) 
This provision provides for the Director General of Taxes to determine the 
tax subject to be a taxpayer, if the tax for the tax object is not yet clear 
 
Example: 
1. 
A tax subject named A who utilizes or uses the land and/or building 
belonging to another person named B not due to a right based on 
the laws or not due to an agreement, in such a case A who utilizes 
or uses the land and/or building is determined to be taxpayer. 
2. 
A tax object that is still in dispute for ownership in the court, then, 
the person or entity that utilizes or uses such a tax object shall be 
determined to be a taxpayer 
3. 
A tax subject has been a long time staying outside the area where 
the tax object is located, whereas the caring for the tax object is 
authorized to a person or entity, then, the authorized person or 
entity can be appointed as a taxpayer. 
The appointment as a taxpayer by the Director General of Taxes is 
not a proof of right ownership 
 
Paragraph (4) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (5) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (6) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (7) 
Based on the provisions in this paragraph, if the Director General of Taxes 
does not give a decision within 1 (one) month as of the date of receipt of 
the statement from the taxpayer, then, the determination as taxpayer 
shall be automatically invalidated and shall be entitled to receive decision 
on revocation of determination as taxpayer. 
 
CHAPTER IV 
TAX RATE 
 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
299 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
Article 5 
 
The tax rate imposed on the tax object is 0.5% (five tenths percent) 
 
Elucidation of Article 5 
Self-explanatory. 
 
CHAPTER V 
IMPOSITION BASE AND HOW TO CALCULATE THE TAX 
 
Article 6 
 
(1) 
The tax imposition base is the Tax Object Sales Value 
(2) 
The amount of the Tax Object Sales Value as referred to in paragraph (1) 
shall be stipulated on three years basis by the Minister of Finance, except 
for certain regions, it shall be stipulated on annual basis in accordance 
with the development of the region. 
(3) 
The tax calculation basis is the Taxable Sales Value that is stipulated at a 
minimum of 20% (twenty percent) and a maximum of 100% (one 
hundred percent) of the tax object sales value. 
(4) 
The amount of Taxable Sales Value percentage as referred to in paragraph 
(3) shall be stipulated by the Government Regulation by taking into 
account the national economic condition 
 
Elucidation of Article 6 
Paragraph (1) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (2) 
Basically, determination of the tax object sales value is once every 3 
(three) years. However, for certain regions which due to the advance of 
the development have resulted in a significant increase in the tax object 
sales value, thus, the sales value is determined once a year 
In determining the sales value, the Minister of Finance shall hear the 
Governor's considerations and shall pay attention to the self-assessment 
principle 
Paragraph (3) 
Taxable Sales Value (assessment value) means a sales value that is used 
as the basis for calculating the tax, namely a certain percentage of the 
actual sales value. 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
300 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
Example: 
1. 
Sales value of a tax object is Rp. 1.000.000,00. Percentage of 
Taxable Sales Value is, for an example 20%, then, the amount of 
the taxable sales value is 20% x Rp. 1.000.000,00 = Rp. 
200.000,00 
2. 
Sales value of a tax object is Rp. 1.000.000,00. Percentage of 
Taxable Sales Value is, for an example 50%, then, the amount of 
the taxable sales value is 50% x Rp. 1.000.000,00 = Rp. 
500.000,00 
Paragraph (4) 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 7 
 
The amount of tax payable is calculated by multiplying the tax rate by the 
Taxable Sales Value 
 
Elucidation of Article 7 
The sales value of the building before applying the tax rate is subtracted 
first by the non-taxable sales value limit of the building of Rp. 
2.000.000,00 (two million rupiah) 
 
Example: 
Taxpayer A has tax objects in the form of: 
- 
Land covering an area of 800 m2 with a selling price of Rp. 
300.000/m2; 
- 
Building covering an area of 400 m2 with a sales value of Rp. 
350.000/m2; 
- 
Luxury garden covering an area of 200 m2 with a sales value of Rp. 
50.000/m2; 
- 
Luxurious fence of 120 m long and the average height of the fence is 
1.5 m with a sales value of Rp. 175.000/m2; 
The percentage of the taxable sales value is, for example 20%. 
 
Total tax payable is as follows: 
1. 
Sales value of the land : 800 x Rp. 300.000,00 = Rp 240.000.000.00 
Sales value of the building 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
301 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
a. 
House and garage 400 x Rp 350.000,00 
= Rp 140.000.000,00 
b. 
Luxury Garden 200 x Rp. 50.000,00 
= Rp   10.000.000,00 
c. 
Luxury fence (120x1.5) x Rp. 175.000,00 = Rp   31.500.000,00 
 
 
= Rp 181.500.000,00 
 
Non-taxable sales value limit of the building 
= Rp    2.000.000,00 
Sales value of the building 
= Rp 179.500.000,00 
Sales value of the land and building 
= Rp 419.500.000,00 
 
2. 
The Amount of the Land and Building Tax payable: 
a. On the land = 0.5% x 20% x Rp. 240.000.000,00 
 
 
 
= Rp 240.000,00 
b. On the building = 0.5% x 20% x Rp. 179.500.000,00 
 
 
 
= Rp 179.500,00 
c. Total tax payable 
= Rp 419.500,00 
 
CHAPTER VI 
FISCAL YEAR, TIME, AND PLACE THAT DETERMINES THE TAX PAYABLE 
 
Article 8 
 
(1) 
The fiscal year is a period of 1 (one) calendar year 
(2) 
Time that determines the tax payable is as per the condition of the tax 
object on 1 January 
(3) 
Place of the tax payable: 
a. 
for the Jakarta area, is in the territory of the Special Capital City of 
Jakarta Province; 
b. 
for other regions, is in the territory of the Regency of the Second 
Level Region or the Municipality of the Second Level Region; which 
covers the location of the tax object 
 
Elucidation of Article 8 
Paragraph (1) 
The period of 1 (one) calendar year is from 1 January up to 31 December 
 
Paragraph (2) 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
302 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
Since the fiscal year begins on 1 January, then, the condition of the tax 
object on that date is the time that determines the tax payable 
Example: 
a. 
A tax object on 1 January 1986 is in the form of land and building. 
On 10 January 1986 the building caught fire, thus, the tax payable 
is remain based on the condition of the tax object as at 1 January 
1986, namely the condition right before the building caught fire; 
b. 
A tax object on 1 January 1986 is in the form of a plot of land 
without any building on it. On 10 August 19, data collection was 
carried out, found that a building had been built on that land, thus, 
the tax payable for 1986 was still subject to tax based on the 
circumstance as at 1 January 1986. 
Whereas, the building would newly be subject to tax in 1987 
Paragraph (3) 
Place of tax payable for Batam Municipality, is in the provincial areas of 
the relevant first level region 
 
CHAPTER VII 
REGISTRATION, TAX OBJECT NOTIFICATION LETTER, NOTIFICATION OF TAX 
DUE, AND TAX ASSESSMENT LETTER 
 
Article 9 
 
(1) 
In term of data collection, the tax subjects are obliged to register their tax 
objects by filling out the Tax Object Notification Letter (Surat Pemberitahuan 
Obyek Pajak) 
(2) 
The Tax Object Notification Letter as referred to in paragraph (1) must be 
filled out clearly, correctly and completely and signed and submitted to 
the Directorate General of Taxes whose working area covers the tax 
object location, not later than 30 (thirty) days as of the receipt date of the 
Tax Object Notification Letter by the tax subject 
(3) 
The implementation and procedures for the tax object registration as 
referred to in paragraphs (1) and (2) shall be further regulated by the 
Minister of Finance 
 
Elucidation of Article 9 
Paragraph (1) 
In term of data collection, the taxpayer will be given with a Tax Object 
Notification Letter to be filled out and returned to the Directorate General 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
303 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
of Taxes. Taxpayer who has ever been subject to IPEDA is not obliged to 
register his/her tax objects unless he/she receives an SPOP, in which case 
he/she is obliged to fill it out and return it to the Directorate General of 
Taxes. 
Paragraph (2) 
Clear, correct and complete means: 
Clear is meant that the writing of the required data in the Tax Object 
Notification Letter (SPOP/ Surat Pemberitahuan Obyek Pajak) shall be made in 
such a way that it does not cause misinterpretation that may inflict a loss 
both to the state and the taxpayer him/herself. 
Correct, means the data reported must conform to the real condition, such 
as the area extent of the land and/or the building, acquisition year and 
price and so on in accordance with the columns/questions in the Tax 
Object Notification Letter (SPOP). 
Paragraph (3) 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 10 
 
(1) 
Based on the Tax Object Notification Letter as referred to in Article 9 
paragraph (1), the Director General of Taxes issues a Notification of Tax 
Due (Surat Pemberitahuan Pajak Terutang) 
(2) 
The Director General of Taxes may issue a Tax Assessment Letter (Surat 
Ketetapan Pajak) in any of the following events: 
a. 
if the Tax Object Notification Letter is not submitted as referred to 
in Article 9 paragraph (2) and after being reprimanded in writing, it 
is not submitted as specified in the Warning Letter; 
b. 
if based on the audit result or other information it turns out that the 
amount payable is higher than the amount of tax calculated based 
on the Tax Object Notification Letter submitted by the taxpayer 
(3) 
The amount of tax payable in the Tax Assessment Letter (Surat Ketetapan 
Pajak) as referred to in paragraph (2) letter a, is the tax principal plus the 
administrative fine of 25% (twenty five percent) calculated from the tax 
principal. 
(4) 
The amount of tax payable in the Tax Assessment Letter as referred to in 
paragraph (2) letter b is the difference between the tax payable based on 
the audit result or other information and the tax payable which is 
calculated based on the Tax Object Notification Letter plus the 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
304 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
administrative fine of 25% (twenty five percent) of the difference of the 
tax payable 
 
Elucidation of Article 10 
Paragraph (1) 
Notification of Tax Due (SPPT) is issued based on the Tax Object Notification 
Letter (SPOP), however, in order to assist the taxpayers, the Notification of Tax 
Due can be issued based on tax object data available in the Directorate General 
of Taxes. 
 
Paragraph (2) 
Provisions in this paragraph authorizes the Directorate General of Taxes to issue 
a Tax Assessment Letter (Surat Ketetapan Pajak/SKP) to the taxpayer who does 
not fulfill the tax obligations properly. 
 
According to the provision in paragraph (2) letter a, a taxpayer who does not 
submit his/her Tax Object Notification Letter on time, even though has been 
reprimanded in writing, and still failing to submit it within the period as specified 
in the Warning Letter, the Director General of Taxes may issue a Tax 
Assessment Letter on in officio basis. On this stipulation, is subject to 
administrative sanction as specified in paragraph (3). 
 
According to the provision in paragraph (2) letter b, if based on the audit result 
or other information available in the Directorate General of Taxes, found that the 
amount of tax payable is higher than the amount of tax in the Notification of Tax 
Due which is calculated based on the Tax Object Notification Letter submitted by 
the taxpayer, the Director General of Taxes issues a tax assessment letter on in 
officio basis. On this stipulation, is subject to administrative sanction as specified 
in paragraph (3) 
 
Paragraph (3) 
This paragraph provides for administrative sanctions to be imposed on taxpayers 
who do not submit the Tax Object Notification Letter as referred to in paragraph 
(2) letter a, such sanctions are imposed in addition to the tax principal, namely 
at 25% (twenty five percent) of the tax principal. 
 
This Tax Assessment Letter, based on data available in the Directorate General 
of Taxes, contains the determination of the tax object and the amount of tax 
payable along with the administrative fine imposed on the taxpayer. 
 
Example:

Taxpayer A does not submit the SPOP 
Based on the available data, the Director General of Taxes issues an SKP which 
contains: 
- tax object with the area extent and the sales value 
- area extent of the tax object according to the SPOP. 
- tax principal 
= Rp 
1.000.000,00

= Rp

- Amount of tax payable in the SKP 
= Rp 
1.250.000,00

This paragraph provides for the administrative sanctions imposed on the 
taxpayer who fills out the Tax Object Notification Letter not in conformity with 
the real condition as referred to in paragraph (2) letter b, namely at 25% 
(twenty five percent) of the difference between the tax payable based on the 
audit result or other information and the tax payable in the Notification of Tax 
Due which is calculated based on the Tax Object Notification Letter submitted by 
the taxpayer 
 
Based on the SPOP, SPPT issued 
= Rp 
1.000.000,00 
Based on the audit, that should be payable in the SKP 
= Rp 
1.500.000,00 
Difference 
= Rp 
500.000,00 
Administrative fine 25% x Rp. 500.000,00 
= Rp

Amount of tax payable in the SKP 
= Rp

As to the amount of tax payable is 
= Rp 
1.000.000,00 
 
The amount specified in the Notification of Tax Due, if not paid yet by the 
taxpayer, its collection shall be carried out based on the Notification of Tax Due. 
 
CHAPTER VIII 
PROCEDURES OF PAYMENT AND BILLING 
 
Article 11 
 
(1) 
Taxes payable based on the Notification of Tax Due as referred to in 
Article 10 paragraph (1) must be paid fully not later than 6 (six) months 
as of the taxpayer’s receipt date of the Notification of Tax Due. 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
306 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
(2) 
Taxes payable based on the Tax Assessment Letter as referred to in 
Article 10 paragraph (3) and paragraph (4) must be paid fully not later 
than 1 (one) month as of the taxpayer’s receipt date of the Tax 
Assessment Letter. 
(3) 
Any Tax payable which, at the maturity of payment, is not paid or 
underpaid, shall be subject to an administrative fine of 2% (two percent) 
per month, which is calculated as from the maturity date until actual 
payment day for a maximum period of 24 (twenty-four) months. 
(4) 
The administrative fine as referred to in paragraph (3) plus the unpaid or 
underpaid tax payable shall be billed with a Tax Collection Letter which 
must be paid fully not later than 1 (one) month as of the taxpayer’s 
receipt date of the Tax Collection Letter. 
(5) 
The Taxes payable shall be paid at Banks, Post and Giro Offices, and other 
places appointed by the Minister of Finance 
(6) 
The procedures for payment and collection as referred to in paragraph 
(1), paragraph (2), paragraph (3), paragraph (4), and paragraph (5) shall 
be regulated by the Minister of Finance 
 
Elucidation of Article 11 
Paragraph (1) 
Example: 
If the SPPT is received by the taxpayer on 1 March  1986, then, its 
payment due date is 31 August 1986 
Paragraph (2) 
Example: 
If the SKP is received by the taxpayer on 1 March  1986 then, its payment 
due date is 31 March 1986 
Paragraph (3) 
According to this provision, the tax payable that is unpaid or underpaid at 
the payment due date, shall be subject to an administrative fine of 2% 
(two percent) per month of the unpaid or underpaid amount for a 
maximum period of 24 (twenty four) months, and a fraction of a month 
shall be calculated as full 1 (one) month 
Example: 
An SPPT of Fiscal year 1986 is received by the taxpayer on 1 March 1986 
with the tax payable amounting to Rp. 100.000,00 (one hundred thousand 
rupiah) 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
307 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
By the taxpayer, it has just been paid on 1 September 1986. Then, such a 
taxpayer is subject to an administrative fine of 2% (two percent), namely: 
2% x Rp. 100.000,00 = Rp 2.000,00. 
 
The tax payable that must be paid on 1 September 1986 is: 
Tax principal + administrative fine = Rp 100.000,00 + Rp. 2.000,00 = Rp 
102.000,00 
 
If such a taxpayer has just paid his/her tax debt on 10 October 1986, 
then the taxpayer is subject to a fine 
2 x 2% of the tax principal, namely: 4% x Rp. 100.000,00 =Rp 4.000,00. 
 
The tax payable that must be paid on 10 October 1986 is: Tax principal + 
administrative fine = Rp 100.000,00 +Rp 4.000,00 = Rp 104.000,00. 
Paragraph (4) 
According to this provision, the administrative fine and the tax principal as 
referred to in the explanatory example of paragraph (3) is billed using a 
Tax Collection Letter (STP) which must be paid fully within one month as 
of the receipt date of the STP. 
Paragraph (5) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (6) 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 12 
 
The Notification of Tax Due, the Tax Assessment Letter, and the Tax Collection 
Letter are the basis for tax collection 
 
Elucidation of Article 12 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 13 
 
The amount of tax that is payable based on the Tax Collection Letter that is not 
paid on time can be billed with a Distress Warrant 
 
Elucidation of Article 13 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
308 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
In case the tax bill payable is paid after the determined maturity, its collection 
shall be carried out by using a distress warrant which is currently based on Law 
Number 19 of 1959 regarding State Tax Collection with Distress Warrant. 
 
Article 14 
 
The Minister of Finance may delegate the authority to collect taxes to the 
Governor the Head of the First Level Region and/or the Regent/Mayor Head of 
the Second Level Region. 
 
Elucidation of Article 14 
The delegation of collection authority to the Governor Head of the First Level 
Region and/or the Regent/Mayor Head of the Second Level Region, is not a 
delegation of collection affairs, but only as a tax collector, whereas the data 
collection of tax objects and the placement of taxes payable shall be remain the 
authority of the Minister of Finance. In the event the amount of tax payable as 
stated in the Notification of Tax Due does not match the tax object in the field, 
the tax collector is not allowed to change the amount of tax payable, but must 
report the same to the Minister of Finance, in this case the Director General of 
Taxes. 
 
CHAPTER IX 
OBJECTION AND APPEAL 
 
Article 15 
 
(1) 
Taxpayer may file objections to the Director General of Taxes on: 
a. 
the Notification of Tax Due; 
b. 
Tax Assessment Letter 
(2) 
Objections shall be submitted in writing in Bahasa Indonesia by stating 
the reasons clearly 
(3) 
Objections must be submitted within 3 (three) months as of the receipt 
date of the letter as referred to in paragraph (1) by the taxpayer, unless 
the taxpayer is able to show that such a period cannot be fulfilled due to 
circumstances beyond his/her control. 
(4) 
The receipt of Objection letter given by an official of the Directorate 
General of Taxes appointed for that purpose and or the receipt of 
Objection Letter by registered post shall be a proof of receipt of the 
Objection Letter for the benefit of the taxpayer. 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
309 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
(5) 
If requested by the taxpayer for the purpose of submission of an 
objection, the Director General of Taxes is obliged to provide in writing the 
matters that become a tax imposition basis 
(6) 
Submission of an objection shall not postpone the obligation to pay taxes 
 
Elucidation of Article 15 
Paragraph (1) 
Any objection to the Notification of Tax Due and the Tax Assessment 
Letter must be submitted each in a separate objection letter for each fiscal 
year. 
Paragraph (2) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (3) 
This provision is intended to give a sufficient time for taxpayers to prepare 
the objection letter and the reasons thereof 
If evident that such a 3 (three) months time limit cannot be fulfilled by 
the taxpayer due to any circumstances beyond his/her control (“force 
major”), then, such a time period may still be considered for extension by 
the Director General of Taxes. 
Paragraph (4) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (5) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (6) 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 16 
 
(1) 
The Director General of Taxes, within not later than 12 (twelve) months 
as of the receipt date of the Objection Letter, shall give a decision on the 
objection filed. 
(2) 
Before issuance of the decision letter, the taxpayer may submit additional 
reasons or a written explanation 
(3) 
The decision of the Director General of Taxes on the objection can take 
the form of accepting in its entirety or in part, declining or increasing the 
amount of the tax payable.

(4) 
In the event the Taxpayer files an objection to the tax assessment letter

(5) 
If the period as referred to in paragraph (1) lapses and the Director 
General of Taxes does not give a decision, such an objection shall be 
deemed accepted. 
 
Elucidation of Article 16 
Paragraph (1) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (2) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (3) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (4) 
This provision requires the taxpayer to prove the incorrectness of the tax 
assessment, in the event the Taxpayer files an objection to the taxes that 
are assessed on in officio basis  
If the Taxpayer is unable to prove the incorrectness of Tax Assessment 
Letter on in officio basis, his/her objection shall be declined 
Paragraph (5) 
This provision is intended to provide legal certainty for the taxpayers, 
namely if within 12 (twelve) months as of the receipt date of the objection 
letter, the Director General of Taxes does not make a decision on the 
objection filed, it means the objection is accepted.

CHAPTER X 
SHARING OF TAX REVENUES 
 
Article 18 
 
(1) 
Tax revenue outcome is the state revenues that are shared between the 
Central Government and the Regional Governments with the share of at 
least 90% (ninety percent) for the Second Level Regional Governments 
and the First Level Regional Governments as income of the relevant 
regions. 
(2) 
Most of the share of Regional Government revenue as referred to in 
paragraph (1) is given to the Second Level Regional Governments 
(3) 
The sharing of the Tax revenue outcome as referred to in paragraph (1) 
and paragraph (2) shall be regulated by the Government Regulation 
 
Elucidation of Article 18 
Paragraph (1) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (2) 
Since this tax revenue is directed to the interests of the people in the 
relevant Second Level Regions, most of this tax revenue is given to the 
Second Level Regions 
Paragraph (3) 
Self-explanatory. 
 
CHAPTER XI 
MISCELLANEOUS 
 
Article 19 
 
(1) 
The Minister of Finance may provide a reduction in the tax payable: 
a. 
due to certain conditions of the tax object that are related to the 
tax subject and/or due to certain other reasons; 
b. 
in the event that the tax object is affected by a natural disaster or 
other extraordinary causes 
(2) 
The provisions on the granting of tax deductions as referred to in 
paragraph (1) shall be regulated by the Minister of Finance

Elucidation of Article 19 
Paragraph (1) 
Letter a 
Certain conditions of the tax object that relate to the tax subject and 
other certain causes, in the form of very limited agricultural land, self-
occupied buildings that are controlled or owned by certain groups of 
taxpayers, lands whose sales value increases as a result of environmental 
changes and positive impacts of the development and its utilization are 
not yet in conformity with the environmental designation 
 
Letter b

Natural disasters mean earthquakes, floods, landslides

Other extraordinary reasons mean, such as:

fires;

droughts;

plant pests

Article 20 
 
At the request of the taxpayer, the Director General of Taxes may deduct the 
administrative fines due to certain matters 
 
Elucidation of Article 20 
This provision gives the taxpayers an opportunity to request for reduction of 
administrative fines as referred to in Article 10 paragraph (3), Article 11 
paragraph (3), and paragraph (4), to the Director General of Taxes. 
The Director General of Taxes may deduct part or all of the administrative fine 
 
Article 21 
 
(1) 
Officials who in their position or in their job duty directly relate with the

Officials who do not fulfill the obligations as referred to in Article 21 shall be 
subject to sanctions in accordance with the prevailing statutory regulations 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
314 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
Elucidation of Article 22 
The statutory regulations applicable to the officials in this article are, among 
others: 
Government Regulation Number 30 of 1980 regarding Disciplinary Regulations 
for Civil Servants, Staatsblad 1860 Number 3 regarding Regulations of Notary 
Position 
 
Article 23 
 
As to any matters that are not specifically regulated in this Law, the provisions in 
Law Number 6 of 1983 regarding the General Provisions and Procedures of 
Taxation shall apply as amended by Law Number 9 of 1994 (State Gazette of 
1994 Number 59, Supplement to State Gazette Number 3566) and other 
statutory regulations. ) 
 
Elucidation of Article 23 
Other statutory regulations mean, among others, Law Number 19 of 1959 
regarding State Tax Collection with Distress Warrant 
 
CHAPTER XII 
CRIMINAL PROVISIONS 
 
Article 24 
 
Any person who, due to his/her negligence: 
a. 
does not return/submit the Tax Object Notification Letter to the 
Directorate General of Taxes; 
b. 
submit the Tax Object Notification Letter, but its contents are incorrect or 
incomplete, and/or attach information containing incorrect contents  
so that results in a loss to the State, shall be sentenced with imprisonment for a 
maximum 1 (one) year and a fine in maximum 2 (two) times the tax payable. 
 
Elucidation of Article 24 
Negligence as referred to in this article means unintentional, negligent, and 
careless thus such an action results in a loss to the state. 
 
 
) : First Amendment (Law Number 12 of 1994) 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
315 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
The Tax Object Notification Letter must be returned/submitted to the Directorate 
General of Taxes not later than 30 days as of the receipt date of the Tax Object 
Notification Letter as referred to in Article 9 paragraph (2) 
 
Article 25 
 
(1) 
Any person who intentionally: 
a. 
does not return/submit the Tax Object Notification Letter to the 
Directorate General of Taxes; 
b. 
submits the Tax Object Notification Letter, but its contents are 
incorrect or incomplete, and/or attach information containing 
incorrect contents;  
c. 
shows false or falsified documents or other documents that are 
false or falsified as if they are true; 
d. 
does not show or does not lend letters or other documents; 
e. 
does not show data or does not provide the required information; 
so that results in a loss to the State, shall be punished with 
imprisonment for a maximum of 2 (two) years or a fine of a 
maximum 5 (five) times the tax payable. 
(2) 
As to the non-taxpayer who takes commits in any actions as referred to in 
paragraph (1) letter d and letter e, shall be sentenced with imprisonment 
for a maximum of 1 (one) year or a fine in maximum of Rp. 2.000.000,- 
(two million rupiah). 
(3) 
The penal section threads as referred to in paragraph (1) is doubled if a 
person commits in another criminal offence in the taxation sector before 
the lapse of 1 (one) year as of completing in undergoing the entire or part 
of the imprisonment sentence awarded or as of the payment of the fine. 
 
Elucidation of Article 25 
Paragraph (1) 
The deeds or actions as referred to in this paragraph that are carried out 
intentionally constitute criminal offenses, therefore threatened with more 
severe imprisonment. 
Paragraph (2) 
Non-taxpayer in this paragraph means an official whose job duty directly 
relates with or has a relationship with the tax objects or other parties 
Paragraph (3) 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
316 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
In order to prevent the repetition of criminal offence in taxation, those 
who commits in another criminal offence as referred to in paragraph (1) 
before the lapse of 1 (one) year as of completing in undergoing the entire 
or part of the imprisonment sentence awarded or as of the payment of the 
fine, shall be punished with more severe penal sanction, namely 2 (two) 
times the imprisonment threat as referred to in paragraph (1) 
 
Article 26 
 
Criminal offences as referred to in Article 24 and Article 25 cannot be prosecuted 
after the lapse of 10 (ten) years as of the expiry of the relevant fiscal year. 
 
Elucidation of Article 26 
Deviation from the provisions in Article 78 of the Criminal Code is intended to 
adjust to the obligation to keep the tax documents for a period of 10 (ten) years 
 
Article 27 
 
Deleted. ) 
 
Elucidation of Article 27 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 28 
 
At to the Regional Development Contribution (Iuran Pembangunan Daerah 
/Ipeda), Wealth Tax (Pajak Kekayaan /PKk), Road Tax and Household Tax (Pajak 
Rumah Tangga /PRT) that are payable for the fiscal year 1985 and earlier, the 
old provisions of tax statutory regulations shall apply until 31 December 1990

As to the tax objects in the oil and gas mining sector as well as in other mining 
sectors, in relation to Work Contracts and Production Sharing Contracts, which 
are still valid when this Law comes into effect, are still subject to the Regional 
Development Contribution (Ipeda) based on the provisions in the Work Contracts 
and the Production Sharing Contracts that are still valid

whereas as to the Work Contracts and the Production Sharing Contracts that 
have been existent, the provisions as specified in the Work Contracts and the 
Production Sharing Contracts shall remain apply

PRESIDENT 
OF THE REPUBLIC OF INDONESIA 
 
COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT OF LAW OF THE REPUBLIC OF 
INDONESIA NUMBER 10 OF 2020 REGARDING STAMP-DUTY 
 
CHAPTER I 
GENERAL PROVISIONS  
Article 1 
 
In this Law, the following definitions apply: 
1. 
Stamp Duty is a tax on Documents 
2. 
Document is something written or a writing, in handwritten, printed, or 
electronic form, which can be used as evidence or certificate 
3. 
Signature is a sign as a symbol of name as usually used, including initial, 
imprint or seal of signature or initial seal or name seal, or other signs in 
lieu of signature, or electronic signature as referred to in the law in the 
field of information and electronic transaction 
4. 
Duty Stamp is a label or strip in affixed, electronic form or other forms 
that has characteristics and contains a security element that is issued by 
the Government of the Republic of Indonesia, used to pay taxes on 
Documents. 
5. 
Indebted Party is a party who is subject to the Stamp Duty and is obliged 
to pay the Stamp Duty payable 
6. 
Postdated Duty Stamping is a duty-stamping that requires legalization 
from an official designated by the Minister 
7. 
Everyone is an individual person and/or entity, whether incorporated or 
unincorporated 
8. 
Minister is the minister who performs government affairs in the financial 
sector 
 
Elucidation of Article 1 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
322 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 2 
 
(1) 
Stamp Duty arrangements are carried out based on the following 
principles: 
a. 
simplicity; 
b. 
efficiency; 
c. 
justice; 
d. 
legal certainty; and 
e. 
benefit 
(2) 
Stamp Duty arrangement is aimed to: 
a. 
maximize the state revenues in order to finance the national 
developments independently towards a prosperous Indonesian 
society; 
b. 
provide legal certainty in the Stamp Duty collection; 
c. 
adapt to the needs of the community; 
d. 
apply the Stamp Duty imposition on a fairer basis; and 
e. 
harmonize the provisions on Stamp Duty with the provisions in 
other statutory regulations 
 
Elucidation of Article 2 
Paragraph (1) 
Letter a 
“Simplicity principle” means the Stamp Duty arrangement must be able to 
provide service convenience to the public in fulfilling their rights and obligations 
Letter b 
“Efficiency principle” means the Stamp Duty arrangement must be oriented to 
minimizing the use of resources to achieve the best work results. 
Letter c 
“Justice principle” means the Stamp Duty arrangement upholds the balance of 
rights and obligations of each of the parties involved 
Letter d 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
323 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
“Legal certainty principle” means the Stamp Duty arrangement must be able to 
realize the orderliness within the community through the guarantee of legal 
certainty 
Letter e 
“Benefit principle” means the Stamp Duty arrangement is beneficial for the 
interests of the state, the nation, and the people, particularly in promoting the 
public welfare. 
Paragraph (2) 
Letter a 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter b 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter c 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter d 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter e 
In relation to development in law, business, and technology, regulations on 
Stamp Duty need to be harmonized with the provisions of the relevant statutory 
regulations, including the statutory regulations in the field of information and 
electronic transaction. 
 
CHAPTER II 
OBJECT, RATE, AND TIME ON WHICH THE STAMP DUTY IS PAYABLE 
 
Part One  
Stamp Duty Object 
 
Article 3 
 
(1) 
Stamp Duty is imposed on: 
a. 
Documents made as a tool to explain a civil incident; and 
b. 
Documents used as evidences in the courts 
(2) 
Civil documents as referred to in paragraph (1) letter a, include: 
a. 
agreements, certificates, statement letters, or other similar letters, 
along with copies there; 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
324 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
b. 
notarial deeds along with grosse, copies, and excepts thereof; 
c. 
deeds of Land Deed Officials along with copies and excerpts 
thereof; 
d. 
securities in any name and in any form; 
e. 
Securities 
transaction 
documents, 
including 
futures 
contract 
transaction documents, in any name and in any form; 
f. 
Auction documents in the form of excerpt of auction minutes, 
auction minutes, copies of auction minutes, and grosse of auction 
minutes; 
g. 
Documents stating AN amount of money with the nominal value 
exceeding Rp. 5.000.000,00 (five million rupiah) which: 
1. 
mention a receipt of money; or 
2. 
contain acknowledgment that the debts have been fully or 
partially paid off or taken in to account; and 
h. 
Other documents stipulated by a Government Regulation 
 
Elucidation of Article 3 
Paragraph (1) 
Letter a 
“Civil event” means any event that falls within the scope of civil law regarding 
people, goods, engagements, evidences, and expiration. 
 
Letter b 
This paragraph is intended to impose Stamp Duty on Documents that are used 
as evidences in the courts, namely: 
1. 
Documents on which Stamp Duty are payable that have not been paid in 
full, including Documents for which the Stamp Duty has not been paid in 
full, but have expired; and 
2. 
Documents which were previously not subject to the Stamp Duty as they 
were not included in the definition of Stamp Duty object based on the 
provisions in this article 
The aforesaid documents must first be treated to be Postdated Duty Stamping 
when they will be used as evidences in the court. This provision confirms that 
the types of Documents can change to be types of Documents as evidence in the 
court due to be used for a purpose that is different from the purpose when the 
Document was created. A document that constitutes a Stamp Duty object for 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
325 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
which the Stamp Duty has been paid in accordance with the provisions in this 
Law, when used as an evidence Document in the court, is no longer obliged to 
be treated to be Postdated Duty Stamping. 
Paragraph (2) 
Letter a 
“Other similar letters” mean letters that are similar to statement letters, such as, 
power of attorney, grant letter, and testaments 
“Copy” means a unit of number of Documents. For example, an agreement 
entered into by 2 (two) parties in 2 (two) copies, then each of the Document is 
subject to a Stamp Duty 
Letter b 
In principle, Stamp Duty as a tax on Document is only imposed 1 (one) time for 
each Document. This means that a grosse, copies, and excerpts of notarial 
deeds are subject to the same Stamp Duty as the original 
“Grosse, copy and excerpt of deed” ha the meaning in conformity with the 
definition as referred to in the law on notary position. 
Letter c 
“Copy of deed” means a verbatim copy of the entire deed and on the bottom of 
the copy of deed is written a phrase “given as a true COPY” (“diberikan sebagai 
SALINAN yang sama bunyinya”) 
“Excerpt of deed” means a verbatim quote of 1 (one) or several parts of a deed 
and on the bottom of the quote of deed is written a phrase “given as an 
EXCERPT” (“diberikan sebagai KUTIPAN”). 
Letter d 
“Securities” mean, among other things, shares, bonds, cheques, bilyet giro, 
acceptances, notes, sukuk, bills of exchange, warrants, options, deposits, and the 
like, including collective share certificates or a group of other securities. For 
example, issuance of 100 (one hundred) shares that is specified in 1 (one) share 
collective certificate, then, the Stamp Duty is only payable on the collective 
share certificate. 
Letter e 
“Securities transaction documents” include, among other things, evidences of 
securities transfer transactions that are carried out on the stock exchange in the 
form of trade confirmations or evidences of other securities transfer transactions 
in any name and in any form, including Documents in the form of notarial deeds, 
official receipts, or other documents, which are used as evidences of securities 
transfer transactions conducted outside the stock exchange 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
326 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
“Futures contract transaction document” means, among other things, an 
evidence of commodity futures transfer contract and securities futures contract 
in any name and in any form, carried out whether on the stock exchange or the 
futures exchange. 
Letter f 
“Excerpt of auction minutes, auction minutes, copy of auction minutes, and 
grosse of auction minutes” has the meaning in conformity with the meaning as 
referred to in statutory regulations in auction sector. 
Letter g 
This amount of money or nominal value also means the amount of money or 
nominal value that is expressed in foreign currency. To determine its rupiah 
value, the amount of money or nominal value is multiplied by the exchange rate 
determined by the Minister, that is applicable when the Document is made so 
that it can be known whether the Document is subject to the Stamp Duty or not. 
Letter h 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 4 
 
Stamp Duty is imposed 1 (one) time for each Document as referred to in Article 
3 
 
Elucidation of Article 4 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Part Two 
Stamp Duty Tariff 
 
Article 5 
 
Documents as referred to in Article 3 are subject to the Stamp Duty at a fixed 
rate of Rp. 10.000,00 (ten thousand rupiah). 
 
Elucidation of Article 5 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 6 
 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
327 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
(1) 
The amount of nominal value limit for a Document to be subject to the 
Stamp Duty as referred to in Article 3 paragraph (2) letter g can be 
reduced or increased in accordance with the national economic condition 
and the people’s income level. 
(2) 
The amount of the Stamp Duty tariff as referred to in Article 5 can be 
reduced or increased in accordance with the national economic condition 
and the people’s income level 
(3) 
Documents as referred to in Article 3 can be subject to the Stamp Duty at 
different fixed rates to the effect of implementing the government 
programs and supporting the implementation of monetary and/or financial 
sector policies. 
(4) 
The change in the nominal value limit for Documents to be subject to the 
Stamp Duty as referred to in paragraph (1), the amount of the Stamp 
Duty tariff as referred to in paragraph (2), or Documents and different 
fixed rates as referred to in paragraph (3), shall be stipulated by a 
Government 
Regulation 
after 
consultation 
with 
the 
House 
of 
Representatives of the Republic of Indonesia 
 
Elucidation of Article 6 
Paragraph (1) 
The national economic condition and the people's income can be shown, among 
other things, from the levels of economic growth, inflation, investment, state 
revenue, and/or people's purchasing power. 
Paragraph (2) 
The national economic condition and the people's income can be shown, among 
other things, from the levels of economic growth, inflation, investment, state 
revenue, and/or people's purchasing power. 
Paragraph (3) 
As an example, the imposition of different fixed rates, such as, on the Securities 
documents, can be subject to the Stamp Duty at a fixed rate that is different 
from the applicable rate based on the need for implementation of financial sector 
policies to the effect of financial inclusion or financial market deepening. 
Paragraph (4) 
“House of Representatives of the Republic of Indonesia” means a commission in 
charge of the finance and banking 
 
Article 7 
 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
328 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
Stamp Duty is not imposed on the Documents that take the form of: 
a. 
documents related to the traffic of people and goods: 
1. 
goods storage letter; 
2. 
bill of lading; 
3. 
passenger and freight transportation documents; 
4. 
evidences for delivery and receipt of goods; 
5. 
delivery letters of goods for sale on account of the sender; and 
6. 
other letters that can be equated with the letters as referred to in 
number 1 up to number 5; 
b. 
all forms of diplomas; 
c. 
receipts of salary payment, waiting fees, pensions, allowances, and other 
payments related to employment relationship, as well as letters submitted 
to obtain the relevant payments; 
d. 
proofs of state money receipt from the state cash account, regional 
government cash account, banks, and other institutions appointed by the 
state based on the provisions of statutory regulations; 
e. 
official receipts for all kinds of taxes and for other equivalent receipts 
originating from the state cash account, regional government cash 
account, banks and other institutions appointed based on the provisions of 
statutory regulations; 
f. 
receipts of money made for internal purposes of organizations; 
g. 
Documents that mention money deposits or securities, payments of 
money deposits to depositors by banks, cooperatives, and other entities 
that organize money deposits, or issuance of securities by custodians to 
customers; 
h. 
mortgage letters; 
i. 
coupons of dividends, interests, or yields from securities, in any name and 
in any form; and 
j. 
Documents issued or produced by Bank Indonesia to the effect of 
implementation of monetary policies 
 
Elucidation of Article 7 
Letter a 
In order to support the people and goods traffic activities, the documents related 
to the traffic of people and goods are not subject to the Stamp Duty. 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
329 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
Number 1 
Self-explanatory. 
Number 2 
Bill of Lading is a ship bill of lading or a certificate (covering letter) of goods 
transported by ship 
Number 3 
Self-explanatory. 
Number 4 
Self-explanatory. 
Number 5 
Self-explanatory. 
Number 6 
“Other letters” mean any letters that are referred to in numbers 1 up to 5. 
However, since its contents and use can be can equated with the relevant 
letters, such letters are not subject to the Stamp Duty. For example, letter of 
deposit of goods, warehouse ceel, and passenger manifest 
Letter b 
Included in the definition of diploma are certificates of study completion, 
certificates of graduation, certificates participation in an education, training, 
course, upgrading, and the like. 
Letter c 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter d 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter e 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter f 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter g 
Documents that mention money deposits include Documents that contain money 
bookkeeping or money deposits in the customer savings in accounts with banks, 
cooperatives, and other entities that manage money deposits and/or contain 
notification of the balance of such deposits. 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
330 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
Documents that mention securities deposits include Documents that contain 
bookkeeping, storage, ownership, or notification of balance of customer's 
securities with the custodians 
“Custodian” means a custodian as referred to in the law in capital market sector 
Examples of money saving Documents with banks include savings and demand 
deposits. Example of Securities deposit documents with the custodians include 
statements of account 
Letter h 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter i 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter j 
“Documents issued or produced by Bank Indonesia to the effect of application of 
monetary policies” mean, among other things, Documents on issuance of Bank 
Indonesia Certificates (SBI) and Bank Indonesia Discount Certificates (SDBI), 
repurchase agreements (Repo) and reverse repurchase agreements of securities, 
Swap Documents including hedging swaps, USD Repo transaction documents, 
Futures export note purchase documents, and futures placement documents 
 
Part Three 
Time When Stamp Duty Payable 
 
Article 8 
 
(1) 
Stamp Duty is payable at the time: 
a. 
The document is affixed with Signature, for: 
1. 
agreements along with copies thereof as referred to in Article 
3 paragraph (2) letter a; 
2. 
notarial deed along with a grosse, copies, and excerpts 
thereof as referred to in Article 3 paragraph (2) letter b; and 
3. 
deed of the Land Deed Official along with copies and excerpts 
thereof as referred to in Article 3 paragraph (2) letter c 
b. 
Documents are completely made, for: 
1. 
securities any name and in any form as referred to in Article 
3 paragraph (2) letter d; and 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
331 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
2. 
Securities transaction documents, including Futures contract 
transaction documents, any name and in any form as 
referred to in Article 3 paragraph (2) letter e 
c. 
Documents are submitted to the party for whom the Documents are 
made, for: 
1. 
certificates, statement letters, or other similar letters, along 
with copies thereof as referred to in Article 3 paragraph (2) 
letter a; 
2. 
tender documents as referred to in Article 3 paragraph (2) 
letter f; and 
3. 
Documents that mentioning the amount of money as referred 
to in Article 3 paragraph (2) letter g 
d. 
Documents are submitted to the court, for Documents for use as 
evidence in the courts as referred to in Article 3 paragraph (1) 
letter b 
e. 
Documents are used in Indonesia, for Documents as referred to in 
Article 3 paragraph (2) that are made overseas 
(2) 
The Minister may determine another time when the Stamp Duty is 
payable 
(3) 
Further provisions on the determination of another time when the Stamp 
Duty is payable as referred to in paragraph (2) shall be regulated in the 
Ministerial Regulation 
 
Elucidation of Article 8 
Paragraph (1) 
Letter a 
The time when the Stamp Duty is payable on a Document that is affixed with 
Signature in this provision is when the Document has been completely made, 
which is closed by affixing the signature of the relevant person. For example, a 
sale and purchase agreement, the Stamp Duty is payable at the time the 
agreement is signed 
Letter b 
The time when the Stamp Duty is payable on a Document as referred to in this 
provision is when the Document is made by the Document issuer. Documents in 
this provision do not involve or require a Signature so that the time when a type 
of Document is payable in this provision occurs when the Document is 
completely made. Determination of the completion in making a Document is 
usually known from the Document date, but can also be known from other signs 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
332 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
that can indicate when the Document is completely made. As an example is a 
trade confirmation of purchase of share securities on the stock exchange in the 
form of electronic document, the Stamp Duty is payable when the trade 
confirmation is made by system by the company. 
Letter c 
The time when the Stamp Duty is payable on the Document as referred to in this 
provision is when the Document is submitted to the party for whom the 
Document is made, not at the time it is signed, for example official receipts, 
checks, and so on. The time when a Document is payable in this provision 
relates with the benefits of a Document that only occurs when it is submitted to 
the party for whom the Document is made 
Letter d 
Self-explanatory. 
Letter e 
“When used in Indonesia” means when the relevant Document is used or 
functioned as a complement or accompaniment for an affair within Indonesian 
jurisdiction. 
For example, an indenture agreement document that is made abroad is used in 
Indonesia when the document is made the basis for debt collection, basis for 
recording or bookkeeping, or an attachment to a report. 
Paragraph (2) 
If in the implementation in the field there are difficulties regarding the 
determination of the time when the Stamp Duty is payable, the Minister may 
determine a time other than that specified in this Law. 
For example, in the event the preparation of a Document in the form of proof of 
transfer of securities cannot be known at the time of completion, then, it can be 
determined another time to determine when the Stamp Duty is payable, for 
example when the contract is signed or when it is recorded in the bookkeeping. 
Paragraph (3) 
Self-explanatory. 
 
CHAPTER III 
INDEBTED PARTY AND COLLECTOR OF THE STAMP DUTY 
 
Part One 
Indebted Party of the Stamp Duty  
 
Article 9 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
333 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
 
(1) 
On a Document which is made unilaterally, the Stamp Duty is payable by 
the party who receives the Document 
(2) 
On a Document which is made by 2 (two) or more parties, the Stamp 
Duty is payable by each of the respective parties on the documents they 
receive 
(3) 
Excluded from the provisions as referred to in paragraph (1) and 
paragraph (2), on Documents in the form of securities as referred to in 
Article 3 paragraph (2) letter d, the Stamp Duty is payable by the 
securities issuer 
(4) 
On a Document which is used as an evidence in the court as referred to in 
Article 3 paragraph (1) letter b, the Stamp Duty is payable by the party 
who submits the Document 
(5) 
On the Documents as referred to in Article 3 which are made overseas and 
used in Indonesia, the Stamp Duty is payable by the beneficiary of the 
Document 
(6) 
Provisions on the Indebted Party as referred to in paragraph (1), 
paragraph (2), paragraph (3), paragraph (4), and paragraph (5) do not 
prevent any party or parties from agreeing or determining the party who 
shall pay the Stamp Duty 
 
Elucidation of Article 9 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Part Two 
Stamp Duty Collector 
 
Article 10 
 
(1) 
Collection of Stamp Duties payable on the Documents as referred to in 
Article 3 paragraph (2) can be carried out by the Stamp Duty collectors 
(2) 
Further provisions on the determination of the Stamp Duty collector as 
referred to in paragraph (1) shall be regulated in the Ministerial 
Regulation 
 
Elucidation of Article 10 
Self-explanatory. 
 
Article 11 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
334 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
 
(1) 
The Stamp Duty Collector is obliged to: 
a. 
collect the Stamp Duties payable on certain Documents from the 
Indebted Party; 
b. 
deposit the Stamp Duty to the state cash account; and 
c. 
report the collection and deposit of Stamp Duty to the office of 
Directorate General of Taxes 
(2) 
The Stamp Duty Collector who does not perform the collection obligations 
as referred to in paragraph (1) letter a and/or letter b, shall be is issued 
with a tax assessment letter in accordance with the provisions of the 
statutory regulations on the general provisions and procedures of 
taxation. 
(3) 
The amount of underpaid Stamp Duty in the tax assessment letter as 
referred to in paragraph (2) is equal to the Stamp Duty that is not 
collected or under-collected and/or is not paid up or underpaid up, plus 
the administrative sanction of 100% (one hundred percent) of the Stamp 
Duty that is not collected or under-collected and/or is not paid up or 
underpaid up 
(4) 
Stamp Duty Collectors who: 
a. 
is late in depositing the Stamp Duty as referred to in paragraph (1) 
letter b; and/or  
b. 
does not report or late to report the collection and deposit of the 
Stamp Duty as referred to in paragraph (1) letter c, 
a tax collection letter shall be issued in accordance with the provisions of 
the statutory regulations on the general provisions and procedures of 
taxation 
(5) 
The provisions on the procedures for collection, deposit, and reporting of 
Stamp Duty as referred to in paragraph (1) are regulated in the Ministerial 
Regulation 
 
Elucidation of Article 11 
Paragraph (1) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (2) 
Self-explanatory. 
Paragraph (3) 

COMPILATION INTO SINGLE DOCUMENT  
Of Law On General Provisions and Procedures of Taxation 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
335 
 
 
 
www.pajak.go.id 
 
 
Example of imposition of Stamp Duty administrative sanctions: 
Based on the audit result of the Stamp Duty collection report, 15 (fifteen) 
Documents of Stamp Duty collection object are found with the following details: 
a. 
1 (one) document has been collected and deposited into the state cash 
account; 
b. 
2 (two) Documents are not collected and not deposited into the state cash 
account; 
c. 
7 (seven) documents have been collected, but not deposited into the state 
cash account; and 
d. 
5 (five) Documents are not collected, but deposited into the state cash 
account 
 
Based on the aforesaid data, the imposition of administrative sanction is 100% 
(one hundred percent) for: 
a. 
2 (two) documents that are not collected and not deposited into the state 
cash account; and 
b. 
7 (seven) documents that have been collected, but not deposited into the 
state cash account. 
 
Whereas, for 1 (one) document that has been collected and deposited into the 
state cash account and 5 (five) documents that are not collected, but deposited 
into the state cash account, are not subject to administrative sanction. 
Thus, calculation in the Underpaid Tax Assessment Letter (SKPKB) is as follows 
Stamp Duty payable 
 
 
Rp150.000,00 
Stamp Duty has been deposited 
1 x Rp10.000,00 
= Rp10.000,00 
5 x Rp10.000,00 
= Rp50.000,00 
 
 
 
Rp 60.000,00 
Stamp Duty underpaid 
 
 
Rp 90.000,00 
Sanction Article 11 paragraph (3) =  
100% x 9 x Rp10.000,00  
 
 
= Rp 90.000,00 
Outstanding Stamp Duty 
 
 
Rp 180.000,00 
 
Paragraph (4) 
Self-explanatory.
